satisfying
\mathcal{U}^{H}\!\left(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{c}_{[S_{1}]}}\!+\!\omega\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}^{H}\right)\mathcal{U}=\operatorname{diag}\!\left\{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{t},0,\ldots,0\right\}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
;
(
131
) follows from Lemma
16
shown below;
(
132
) holds because
\prod_{i=1}^{t}\lambda_{i}=\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\omega\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}^{H}\right|
.
Together with (
128
), we can obtain

q_{4,t}\left(\omega,\nu\right)\leq\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}}\!\!\left[\frac{\gamma\!\left(tL,L\left({t(1+\eta)-n\nu+n(1+\delta)(1-\omega)}\right)\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\omega\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}^{H}\right|^{-\frac{1}{t}}\right)}{\Gamma\left(tL\right)}\right].

(133)

Lemma 16
(
[
45
]
)

Assume
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
are independently distributed chi-square variables with
m
degrees of freedom. Assume
\tilde{x}\sim\chi^{2}\left(sm\right)
.
Let the constant
\gamma_{j}>0
. Then, for every constant
c
,

\mathbb{P}\left({\sum}_{j=1}^{s}\gamma_{j}x_{j}<c\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left({\prod}_{j=1}^{s}\gamma_{j}^{\frac{1}{s}}\tilde{x}<c\right).

(134)

Substituting (
123
), (
126
), and (
133
) into (
122
), we can obtain an upper bound on
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}^{c}\right]
in the case of
t<n
and
\omega\in(0,1]
as presented in (
24
).

Case 2:

t\geq n,\omega\in(0,1]
.

Next, we upper-bound
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}^{c}\right]
when
t\geq n
and
\omega\in(0,1]
.
Recall that
\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}=\mathbf{z}_{l}+\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}\mathbf{h}_{l}
.
We define the event
\mathcal{G}_{\eta}=\left\{\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left\|\mathbf{z}_{l}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq nL(1+\eta)\right\}
for
\eta\geq 0
.
We can bound
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}^{c}\right]
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcup_{S_{1}}\left\{\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left\|\mathbf{z}_{l}\right\|_{2}^{2}>\omega\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}\right\|_{2}^{2}+nL\nu\right\}\bigcap\mathcal{G}_{\eta}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\eta}^{c}\right]

(135)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcup_{S_{1}}\left\{\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}\right\|_{2}^{2}<nLC_{\omega,\nu,\eta}\right\}\right]+1-\frac{\gamma\left(nL,nL\left(1+\eta\right)\right)}{\Gamma\left(nL\right)}

(136)

\displaystyle\!\leq{\binom{K_{a}}{t}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}}\!\!\left[\frac{\gamma\!\left(nL,nLC_{\omega,\nu,\eta}\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}\!+\!\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}{\tilde{\mathbf{A}}}_{S_{1}}^{H}\right|^{-\frac{1}{n}}\right)}{\Gamma\left(nL\right)}\right]\!+1-\frac{\gamma\left(nL,nL\left(1+\eta\right)\right)}{\Gamma\left(nL\right)},

(137)

where
C_{\omega,\nu,\eta}=\frac{1+\eta-\nu}{\omega}
.
Here, (
137
) follows by applying Lemma
16
and from the fact that
\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}\sim\mathcal{CN}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{n}+\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}^{H}\right)
conditioned on
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{S_{1}}
.

Case 3:

\omega=0
.

In the case of
\omega=0
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}^{c}\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left\|\mathbf{z}_{l}\right\|_{2}^{2}>nL\nu\right]=1-\frac{\gamma\left(nL,nL\nu\right)}{\Gamma\left(nL\right)}.

(138)

In conclusion, based on Fano’s bounding technique, we have obtained
q_{1,t}\left(\omega,\nu\right)
in (
21
) (i.e. an upper bound on the first term in (
105
)) and
q_{2,t}\left(\omega,\nu\right)
in (
24
) (i.e. an upper bound on the second term in (
105
)),
which contributes to an upper bound
p_{1,t}
in (
20
) on the probability
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]
.

B-B

Upper-bounding
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]
based on Gallager’s bounding technique

Let
\mathbf{H}_{1}
and
\mathbf{H}_{2}
be
t\times L
submatrices of
\mathbf{H}
formed by rows corresponding to the support of
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
, respectively.
Let
\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}
and
\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{S_{2}}
be
n\times t
submatrices of
\mathbf{A}
formed by columns corresponding to the codewords transmitted by users in the set
S_{1}
and the codewords not transmitted but decoded for users in the set
S_{2}
, respectively.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left.\mathcal{F}_{t}\right|\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2},\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\!\left[\left.\bigcup_{S_{1}}\bigcup_{S_{2}}\bigcup_{\mathbf{c}^{{}^{\prime}}_{[S_{2}]}}\!\left\{\sum_{l\in[L]}\left\|\mathbf{z}_{l}+\!\sum_{k\in S_{1}}\!h_{k,l}\mathbf{c}_{(k)}-\!\sum_{k\in S_{2}}\!h_{k,l}\mathbf{c}^{\prime}_{(k)}\right\|_{2}^{2}\!\leq\!\sum_{l\in[L]}\left\|\mathbf{z}_{l}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\right|\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2},\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\right]

(139)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S_{1}}\sum_{S_{2}}M^{\rho t}\left(\mathbb{P}\left[\left.\left\|\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{1}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{S_{2}}\mathbf{H}_{2}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathbf{Z}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right|\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2},\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\right]\right)^{\rho},

(140)

where (
140
) follows by applying Gallager’s
\rho
-trick, i.e.,
\mathbb{P}\left[\cup_{j}B_{j}\right]\leq\left(\sum_{j}\mathbb{P}\left[B_{j}\right]\right)^{\rho}
for any
\rho\in[0,1]

[
31
]
,
[
46
, Section 5.6]
.

The probability on the RHS of (
140
) can be upper-bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left.\left\|\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{1}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{S_{2}}\mathbf{H}_{2}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathbf{Z}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right|\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2},\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{\beta\left\|\mathbf{Z}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{S_{2}}^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\left.\exp\left\{-\beta\left\|\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{1}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{S_{2}}\mathbf{H}_{2}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right\}\right|\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2},\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\right]

(141)

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{\beta\left\|\mathbf{Z}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right\}\left|\mathbf{I}_{2L}+\beta\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right|^{-\frac{n}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\exp\left\{-\beta\tilde{\bm{\mu}}_{i}^{T}\left(\mathbf{I}_{2L}+\beta\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\},

(142)

where (
141
) follows from the Chernoff bound in Lemma
14
with
\beta\geq 0
,
and (
142
) is obtained as follows.
Let
\bm{\mu}_{i}=\left([\mathbf{Z}]_{i,:}+\left[\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\right]_{i,:}\mathbf{H}_{1}\right)^{H}
and
\bm{\nu}_{i}=\bm{\mu}_{i}-\left(\left[\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{S_{2}}\right]_{i,:}\mathbf{H}_{2}\right)^{H}
.
Conditioned on
\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2}
, and
\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}
, we have
\bm{\nu}_{i}\sim\mathcal{CN}\left(\bm{\mu}_{i},P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{2}\right)
for
i\in[n]
.
Let
\tilde{\bm{\nu}}_{i}=\begin{bmatrix}\Re\left(\bm{\nu}_{i}\right)\\
\Im\left(\bm{\nu}_{i}\right)\end{bmatrix}
,

\tilde{\bm{\mu}}_{i}=\begin{bmatrix}\Re\left(\bm{\mu}_{i}\right)\\
\Im\left(\bm{\mu}_{i}\right)\end{bmatrix}
,
and
\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}=\begin{bmatrix}\Re(P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{2})&-\Im(P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{2})\\
\Im(P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{2})&\Re(P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{2})\end{bmatrix}
.
We have
\tilde{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\tilde{\bm{\mu}}_{i},\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right)
conditioned on
\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2}
, and
\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}
.
Then, applying Lemma
15
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{S_{2}}^{{}^{\prime}}}\!\!\left[\left.\exp\!\left\{-\beta\bm{\nu}_{i}^{H}\bm{\nu}_{i}\right\}\right|\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2},\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{S_{2}}^{{}^{\prime}}}\left[\left.\exp\left\{-\beta\tilde{\bm{\nu}}_{i}^{T}\tilde{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\right\}\right|\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2},\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\right]

(143)

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbf{I}_{2L}\!+\!\beta\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\!\exp\!\left\{\!-\beta\tilde{\bm{\mu}}_{i}^{T}\!\left(\mathbf{I}_{2L}\!+\!\beta\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right)^{-1}\!\tilde{\bm{\mu}}_{i}\right\},

(144)

which yields (
142
).

Substituting (
142
) into (
140
) and taking the expectation over
\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}
and
\mathbf{Z}
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left.\mathcal{F}_{t}\right|\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S_{1}}\sum_{S_{2}}M^{\rho t}\underbrace{\left|\mathbf{I}_{2L}\!+\!\beta\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right|^{-\frac{\rho n}{2}}\left|\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{I}_{2L}\!+\!\rho\beta\left(\mathbf{I}_{2L}\!+\!\beta\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathbf{I}_{2L}\!+\!\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}\right)&\rho\beta\!\left(\mathbf{I}_{2L}\!+\!\beta\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right)^{-1}\\
-\rho\beta\mathbf{I}_{2L}&\left(1-\rho\beta\right)\mathbf{I}_{2L}\end{bmatrix}\right|^{-\frac{n}{2}}}_{C_{S_{1},S_{2}}},

(145)

where
\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}=\begin{bmatrix}\Re(P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{1})&-\Im(P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{1})\\
\Im(P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{1})&\Re(P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{1})\end{bmatrix}
.
Here, (
145
) follows by applying Lemma
15
and Sylvester’s determinant theorem under the condition that
0\leq\beta<1/{\rho}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle C_{S_{1},S_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left|\left(1-\rho\beta\right)\mathbf{I}_{2L}\right|^{-\frac{n}{2}}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\cdot\left|\left(\mathbf{I}_{2L}\!+\!\beta\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right)^{\!\rho}+\rho\beta\left(\mathbf{I}_{2L}\!+\!\beta\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right)^{\!\rho-1}\!\left(\mathbf{I}_{2L}\!+\!\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}\right)+\frac{\rho^{2}\beta^{2}}{1-\rho\beta}\left(\mathbf{I}_{2L}\!+\!\beta\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right)^{\!\rho-1}\right|^{-\frac{n}{2}}

(146)

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbf{I}_{2L}+\beta\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right|^{-\frac{(-1+\rho)n}{2}}\left|\mathbf{I}_{2L}+\rho\beta\left(1-\rho\beta\right)\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{1}+\beta\left(1-\rho\beta\right)\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{2}\right|^{-\frac{n}{2}}

(147)

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbf{I}_{L}\!+\!\beta P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{2}\right|^{(1-\rho)n}\left|\mathbf{I}_{L}+\rho\beta\left(1\!-\!\rho\beta\right)P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{1}+\beta\left(1\!-\!\rho\beta\right)P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{2}\right|^{-{n}}\!,

(148)

where (
146
) holds because
\left|\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}&\mathbf{B}\\
\mathbf{C}&\mathbf{D}\end{bmatrix}\right|=\left|\mathbf{D}\right|\left|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\mathbf{C}\right|
when
\mathbf{D}
is nonsingular, and (
148
) holds because
\left|\mathbf{D}\right|^{2}=\left|\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\Re{\left(\mathbf{D}\right)}&-\Im{\left(\mathbf{D}\right)}\\
\Im{\left(\mathbf{D}\right)}&\Re{\left(\mathbf{D}\right)}\end{array}\right]\right|
.

Substituting (
148
) into (
145
) and taking the expectation over
\mathbf{H}_{1}
and
\mathbf{H}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t_{0}=0}^{t}{\binom{K_{a}}{t}}{\binom{t}{t_{0}}}{\binom{K-K_{a}}{t-t_{0}}}M^{\rho t}\;\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2}}\left[\exp\left\{(1-\rho)n\ln\left|\mathbf{I}_{L}+\beta P^{\prime}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{2}\right|\right.\right.

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\left.\left.-n\ln\left|\mathbf{I}_{L}+\beta\left(1-\rho\beta\right)P^{\prime}\left(\rho\mathbf{H}_{1}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{1}+\mathbf{H}_{2}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{2}\right)\right|\right\}\right],

(149)

where (
149
) holds because the expectation is unchanged for different
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
once
t_{0}
and
t
are fixed, considering that channel coefficients are i.i.d. for different users.
Taking the minimum over
\rho
and
\beta
on the RHS of (
149
), we obtain an upper bound on
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]
based on Gallager’s
\rho
-trick, which is denoted as
p_{2,t}
in (
1
).
This completes the proof of Theorem
1
.

Appendix C
Proof of Corollary
3

In a special case where all users are active (i.e.
K_{a}=K
), the set
{S}_{1}
of misdecoded users is the same as the set
{S}_{2}
including detected users with false alarm codewords.
Thus,
\tilde{p}_{1,t}
can be easily obtained from Theorem
1
and its proof is omitted here for the sake of brevity.
Moreover, the proof of
\tilde{p}_{2,t}
is provided in Appendix
C-A
; the upper bound
\tilde{p}_{2,t}^{\text{u}}
on
\tilde{p}_{2,t}
is derived in Appendix
C-B
.

C-A

Proof of (
30
)

In a special case where all users are active, i.e.,
K_{a}=K
,
\mathbf{H}_{1}=\mathbf{H}_{2}
, and
S_{1}=S_{2}
, it is easy to see that the optimum value of
\beta
minimizing (
149
) is given by
\beta^{*}=1/(1+\rho)
. Then, we have

\mathbb{P}\left[\left.\mathcal{F}_{t}\right|\mathbf{H}_{1}\right]\leq\sum_{S_{1}}M^{\rho t}\exp\left\{-\rho n\ln\left|\mathbf{I}_{L}+\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{1}\right|\right\}.

(150)

Taking the expectation over
\mathbf{H}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\min_{0\leq\rho\leq 1}\sum_{S_{1}}M^{\rho t}\;\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H}_{1}}\!\left[\exp\left\{-\rho n\ln\left|\mathbf{I}_{L}+\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{1}\right|\right\}\right]

(151)

\displaystyle=\min_{0\leq\rho\leq 1}{\binom{K}{t}}M^{\rho t}\;\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{G}}\!\left[\exp\left\{-L\ln\left|\mathbf{I}_{t}+\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{G}}^{H}\right|\right\}\right],

(152)

where (
152
) holds when
\rho n
is an integer and each element of
\mathbf{H}_{1}\in\mathbb{C}^{t\times L}
and
\mathbf{G}\in\mathbb{C}^{t\times\rho n}
is i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}\left(0,1\right)
distributed. This is because

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{G}}\!\!\left[\exp\!\left\{-\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}\left\|\mathbf{G}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{1}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right\}\right]\!

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H}_{1}}\!\!\left[\prod_{i=1}^{\rho n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\exp\!\left\{-\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}\left\|\left([\mathbf{G}]_{:,i}\right)^{H}\!\mathbf{H}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\right|\mathbf{H}_{1}\right]\right]

(153)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H}_{1}}\!\left[\exp\left\{-\rho n\ln\left|\mathbf{I}_{L}+\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}{\mathbf{H}}_{1}^{H}{\mathbf{H}}_{1}\right|\right\}\right]

(154)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{G}}\!\left[\prod_{l=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\exp\left\{-\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}\left\|\mathbf{G}^{H}\left[\mathbf{H}_{1}\right]_{:,l}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\right|\mathbf{G}\right]\right]

(155)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{G}}\!\left[\exp\left\{-L\ln\left|\mathbf{I}_{t}+\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{G}}^{H}\right|\right\}\right],

(156)

where (
154
) and (
156
) follows from Lemma
15
.
Denote the RHS of (
152
) as
\tilde{p}_{2,t}
. This completes the proof of (
30
).

C-B

Proof of the upper bound
\tilde{p}_{2,t}^{\text{u}}
on
\tilde{p}_{2,t}

Recall that each element of
\mathbf{G}\in\mathbb{C}^{t\times\rho n}
is i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}\left(0,1\right)
distributed.
In the case of
\rho n\geq t+L
, the expectation in (
30
) can be upper-bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{G}}\!\left[\left|\mathbf{I}_{t}+\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{G}}^{H}\right|^{-L}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}\right)^{-Lt}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{G}}\!\left[\left|{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{G}}^{H}\right|^{-L}\right]

(157)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}\right)^{-Lt}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=\rho n-t+1}^{\rho n}\left(\frac{\chi^{2}(2i)}{2}\right)^{-L}\right]

(158)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}\right)^{-Lt}\prod_{i=\rho n-t+1}^{\rho n}\frac{\Gamma(i-L)}{\Gamma(i)},

(159)

where (
157
) follows because
\left|\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{A}\right|\geq\left|\mathbf{A}\right|
when
\mathbf{A}
is a positive semidefinite matrix;
(
158
) follows because the determinant of the Wishart matrix
\left|{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{G}}^{H}\right|
has the same distribution as the product of independent random variables with chi-square distributions, i.e.,
\prod_{i=\rho n-t+1}^{\rho n}\frac{\chi^{2}(2i)}{2}

[
47
, Section 3.5]
;
(
159
) follows from the moments of chi-square random variables.

Applying similar ideas, we can upper-bound this term in the case of
\rho n\leq t-L
as follows:

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{G}}\!\left[\left|\mathbf{I}_{t}+\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{G}}^{H}\right|^{-L}\right]\leq\left(\frac{P^{\prime}}{1+\rho}\right)^{-L\rho n}\prod_{i=t-\rho n+1}^{t}\frac{\Gamma(i-L)}{\Gamma(i)}.

(160)

Substituting (
159
) and (
160
) into (
30
) and considering
\tilde{p}_{2,t}\leq 1
, we can obtain (
31
).

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
4

In this appendix, we prove Theorem
4
to establish a converse bound on the minimum required energy-per-bit for the CSIR case.
We assume a genie
G
reveals the set
\mathcal{K}_{a}
of active users and a subset
S_{1}\subset\mathcal{K}_{a}
for messages
\mathcal{W}_{S_{1}}=\left\{W_{k}:k\in{S_{1}}\right\}
and corresponding fading coefficients to the decoder.
It is evident that a converse bound in the genie case is a converse bound for the problem without genie.
Let
S_{2}=\mathcal{K}_{a}\backslash S_{1}
of size
t
.
Let
\bm{\Phi}_{S_{2}}\in\{0,1\}^{MK\times K}
denote which codewords are transmitted by users in the set
S_{2}\subset\mathcal{K}_{a}
, where
\left[\bm{\Phi}_{S_{2}}\right]_{(k-1)M+W_{k},k}=1
if the
k
-th user belonging to the set
S_{2}
is active and the
W_{k}
-th codeword is transmitted, and
\left[\bm{\Phi}_{S_{2}}\right]_{(k-1)M+W_{k},k}=0
otherwise.
The equivalent received signal of the
l
-th antenna at the BS is given by

\mathbf{y}_{l}^{G}=\sum_{k\in{S_{2}}}{h}_{k,l}\mathbf{x}_{(k)}+\mathbf{z}_{l}=\mathbf{X}{\bm{\Phi}}_{S_{2}}\mathbf{h}_{l}+\mathbf{z}_{l}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}.

(161)

The equivalent received message over all antennas is given by

\mathbf{Y}^{G}=\mathbf{X}{\bm{\Phi}}_{S_{2}}\mathbf{H}+\mathbf{Z},

(162)

where
\mathbf{Y}^{G}=[\mathbf{y}_{1}^{G},\mathbf{y}_{2}^{G},\ldots,\mathbf{y}_{L}^{G}]\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times L}
, and
\mathbf{H}
and
\mathbf{Z}
are defined in Section
II
.
Denote the decoded signal for the
k
-th user with genie as
\hat{W}_{k}^{G}
.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{k}=1\left[{W}_{k}\neq\hat{W}_{k}^{G}\right]
and
P_{e,k}^{G}=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{M}_{k}\right]
. We have
P_{e,k}^{G}=0
for
k\in S_{1}
.
The averaged PUPE is
P_{e}^{G}=\frac{1}{K_{a}}\sum_{k\in{S_{2}}}P_{e,k}^{G}\leq\epsilon
.

Based on the Fano inequality, we have

\frac{t}{K_{a}}J-P_{e}^{G}\log_{2}\left(2^{J}-1\right)-\frac{1}{K_{a}}\sum_{k\in S_{2}}h_{2}\left(P_{e,k}^{G}\right)\leq\frac{1}{K_{a}}\sum_{k\in S_{2}}I_{2}\left(W_{k};\hat{W}_{k}^{G}\right).

(163)

Considering the concavity of
h_{2}(\cdot)
and the inequality that
P_{e}^{G}\leq\epsilon\leq 1-\frac{1}{2^{J}}
, we have

P_{e}^{G}\log_{2}\left(2^{J}-1\right)+\frac{1}{K_{a}}\sum_{k\in S_{2}}h_{2}\left(P_{e,k}^{G}\right)\leq\epsilon J+h_{2}\left(\epsilon\right).

(164)

Denote
\mathcal{W}_{S_{2}}=\left\{W_{k}:k\in{S_{2}}\right\}
,

\mathcal{X}_{S_{2}}=\left\{\mathbf{x}_{(k)}:k\in{S_{2}}\right\}
, and
\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{S_{2}}^{G}=\left\{\hat{W}_{k}^{G}:k\in{S_{2}}\right\}
.
The matrix
\mathbf{H}_{t}
is a
t\times L
submatrix of
\mathbf{H}
corresponding to fading coefficients of users in the set
S_{2}
.
We can upper-bound
\sum_{k\in S_{2}}I_{2}\left(W_{k};\hat{W}_{k}^{G}\right)
as

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in S_{2}}I_{2}\left(W_{k};\hat{W}_{k}^{G}\right)

\displaystyle=H_{2}\left(\mathcal{W}_{S_{2}}\right)-\sum_{k\in S_{2}}H_{2}\left(W_{k}\left|\hat{W}_{k}^{G}\right.\right)

(165)

\displaystyle\leq H_{2}\left(\mathcal{W}_{S_{2}}\right)-H_{2}\left(\mathcal{W}_{S_{2}}\left|\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{S_{2}}^{G}\right.\right)

(166)

\displaystyle=I_{2}\left(\mathcal{W}_{S_{2}};\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{S_{2}}^{G}\right)

(167)

\displaystyle\leq I_{2}\left(\mathcal{X}_{S_{2}};\mathbf{Y}^{G}\right)

(168)

\displaystyle\leq n\;\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\!\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{L}+P\mathbf{H}_{t}^{H}\mathbf{H}_{t}\right|\right],

(169)

where
H_{2}(x)
denotes the entropy of a random variable
x
. Here, (
166
) follows because

H_{2}\left(\mathcal{W}_{S_{2}}\left|\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{S_{2}}^{G}\right.\right)=\sum_{k\in S_{2}}H_{2}\left(W_{k}\left|\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{S_{2}}^{G},{W}_{1},\ldots,{W}_{k-1}\right.\right)\leq\sum_{k\in S_{2}}H_{2}\left(W_{k}\left|\hat{W}_{k}^{G}\right.\right),

(170)

(
168
) follows due to the data processing inequality and the Markov chain:
\mathcal{W}_{S_{2}}\to\mathcal{X}_{S_{2}}\to\mathbf{Y}^{G}\to\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{S_{2}}^{G}
,
and (
169
) holds because both
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{Z}
are independent for
n
channel uses and
the normal distribution of codewords maximizes the entropy for a given variance
[
3
, Theorem 8.6.5]
.

Substituting (
164
) and (
169
) into (
163
), we can obtain (
34
) in Theorem
4
.
Then, applying the concavity of
\log_{2}\left|\cdot\right|
function, we obtain (
35
), which completes the proof of Theorem
4
.

Appendix E
Proof of Theorem
5

To prove Theorem
5
, we first establish an achievability result in Appendix
E-A
and then prove a converse result in Appendix
E-B
for the CSIR case assuming all users are active, i.e.
K=K_{a}
.

E-A

Achievability

In a special case where all users are active, the PUPE can be upper-bounded as

\displaystyle P_{e}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}1\left[W_{k}\neq\hat{W}_{k}\right]\right]_{\text{no power constraint}}+\tilde{p}_{0}

(171)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{1}+\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}1\left[W_{k}\neq\hat{W}_{k}\right]\geq\epsilon_{1}\right]_{\text{no power constraint}}+\tilde{p}_{0}

(172)

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{1}+\sum_{t=\lceil\epsilon_{1}K\rceil}^{K}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]_{\text{no power constraint}}+\tilde{p}_{0},

(173)

where
\epsilon_{1}
is a positive constant less than
\epsilon
;
\mathcal{F}_{t}=\left\{\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}1\left\{W_{k}\neq\hat{W}_{k}\right\}=t\right\}
denotes the event that there are exactly
t
misdecoded users;

\tilde{p}_{0}
upper-bounds the power constraint violation probability given by

\tilde{p}_{0}=K\;\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{x}{2n}>\frac{P}{{P}^{\prime}}\right]\leq K\exp\left\{-n\left(\frac{P}{{P}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{2\frac{P}{{P}^{\prime}}-1}\right)\right\},\;\;x\sim\chi^{2}(2n),

(174)

which follows from Lemma
17
presented below.
It is easy to see that
c_{P}=\frac{P}{{P}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{2\frac{P}{{P}^{\prime}}-1}
is a positive finite constant, provided that
\frac{P}{P^{\prime}}-1
is a positive finite constant.
In the case of
\ln K=o(n)
, we have
\tilde{p}_{0}\leq\exp\left\{o(n)-c_{P}n\right\}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.

Lemma 17
(
[
48
]
)

Let
x\sim\chi^{2}\left(m\right)
be a central chi-square distributed variable with
m
degrees of freedom. For
\forall a>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left[x-m\geq a\right]\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2}\left(a+m-\sqrt{m}\sqrt{2a+m}\right)\right\}.

(175)

An upper bound
\tilde{p}_{1,t}
on
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]_{\text{no power constraint}}
is given in Corollary
3
.
Next, we pay attention to upper-bounding
\tilde{p}_{1,t}
, thereby finding the condition under which
\sum_{t=\lceil\epsilon_{1}K\rceil}^{K}\tilde{p}_{1,t}\to 0
and thus
P_{e}\leq\epsilon
.
In the case of
\epsilon_{1}K\geq n+L
, for
t=\lceil\epsilon_{1}K\rceil,\lceil\epsilon_{1}K\rceil+1,\ldots,K
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{p}_{1,t}

\displaystyle\leq{\binom{K}{t}}M^{t}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}}{2}\right)^{-Ln}\prod_{i=t-n+1}^{t}\frac{\Gamma(i-L)}{\Gamma(i)}

(176)

\displaystyle\leq{\binom{K}{t}}M^{t}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}\left(t-n+1-L\right)}{2}\right)^{-Ln},

(177)

where (
176
) follows from (
31
) and (
32
) by allowing
\rho=1
, and (
177
) follows from the equality that
\Gamma(x)=(x-1)!
for any positive integer
x
.

Let
t=\theta K
with
\theta\in S_{\theta}=\left\{\frac{1}{K},\frac{2}{K},\ldots,1\right\}\cap\left[\epsilon_{1},1\right]
. We have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=\lceil\epsilon_{1}K\rceil}^{K}\tilde{p}_{1,t}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=\lceil\epsilon_{1}K\rceil}^{K}{\binom{K}{t}}M^{t}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}\left(t-n+1-L\right)}{2}\right)^{-Ln}

(178)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\!\left\{o\!\left(K\right)+\!K\max_{\theta\in S_{\theta}}\left\{h(\theta)+\theta\ln M-\!\frac{Ln}{K}\ln\left(\frac{P^{\prime}\!\left(\theta K-n+1-L\right)}{2}\right)\right\}\right\}

(179)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\!\left\{o\!\left(K\right)+\!K\!\left(h\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)+\ln M-\frac{Ln}{K}\ln\left(\frac{P^{\prime}\left(\epsilon_{1}K-n+1-L\right)}{2}\right)\right)\right\},

(180)

where (
179
) follows from the inequality that
[
3
, Example 11.1.3]

\binom{K}{t}\leq\exp\left\{Kh(\theta)\right\}.

(181)

Therefore, in the case of
K\to\infty
, we have
\sum_{t=\lceil\epsilon_{1}K\rceil}^{K}\tilde{p}_{1,t}\to 0
provided that the finite constant

c_{1}=\frac{Ln}{K}\ln\left(\frac{P^{\prime}\left(\epsilon_{1}K-n+1-L\right)}{2}\right)-h\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)-\ln M>0.

(182)

Assume
M=\Theta(1)
,
K
and
n\to\infty
, and
K\gg\frac{n+L-1}{\epsilon_{1}}
. In the case of
KP^{\prime}=\Omega(1)
, we can obtain that (
182
) is satisfied if and only if
\frac{nL\ln KP^{\prime}}{K}=\Omega\left(1\right)
, which can be divided into the following two relations:

1.

We assume
P^{\prime}K
is a finite positive constant satisfying
P^{\prime}K>\frac{2}{\epsilon_{1}}
. In this case, we have

c_{1}=c_{3}\frac{nL}{K}-c_{2},

(183)

where
c_{2}=h\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)+\ln M
and
c_{3}
is a finite positive constant.
In order to satisfy the condition in (
182
), it is possible to choose
\frac{nL}{K}=\Omega\left(1\right)
and
P^{\prime}K=\Theta\left(1\right)
.
An example for this case is that the number of BS antennas satisfies
L=\Theta\left(n\right)
, the power satisfies
P^{\prime}=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)
and the number of users satisfies
K=\Theta(n^{2})
.

2.

In the case of
P^{\prime}K\to\infty
, we have

c_{1}={Ln}\frac{\ln KP^{\prime}}{K}-{Ln}\frac{\mathcal{O}(1)}{K}-c_{2},

(184)

where
c_{2}=h\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)+\ln M
. Applying (
184
), in order to satisfy the condition in (
182
), it is possible to choose
\frac{nL\ln KP^{\prime}}{K}=\Omega\left(1\right)
with
KP^{\prime}\to\infty
.
An example for this case satisfies
L=\Theta\left(\frac{n}{\ln n}\right)
,
P^{\prime}=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)
, and
K=\Theta(n^{2})
.

Combining (
174
) and (
182
), we conclude that assuming
K,n\to\infty
,
\ln K=o(n)
,
KP=\Omega(1)
,
M=\Theta(1)
, and
K\geq\frac{n+L-1}{\epsilon_{1}}
, the PUPE requirement
P_{e}\leq\epsilon
is satisfied provided that
\frac{nL\ln KP}{K}=\Omega\left(1\right)
.
In particular, the PUPE requirement is satisfied for
K=\frac{n+L-1}{\epsilon_{1}}
users when
KP=\Omega(1)
(the condition
\frac{nL\ln KP}{K}=\Omega\left(1\right)
is satisfied directly in this case).
It was proved in
[
8
, Appendix A-C]
that, if one can achieve a certain PUPE for
K
users, it will also be possible to achieve the same PUPE for less than
K
users.
Thus, one can reliably serve
K\leq\frac{n+L-1}{\epsilon_{1}}
users provided that
\frac{n+L-1}{\epsilon_{1}}P=\Omega(1)
, or under a stricter condition that
KP=\Omega(1)
.
As a result, assuming
K,n\to\infty
,
\ln K=o(n)
,
KP=\Omega(1)
, and
M=\Theta(1)
, the PUPE requirement
P_{e}\leq\epsilon
is satisfied if
\frac{nL\ln KP}{K}=\Omega\left(1\right)
.
That is, it is possible to choose the following two regimes:
\frac{nL}{K}=\Omega\left(1\right)
and
KP=\Theta\left(1\right)
;

\frac{nL\ln KP}{K}=\Omega\left(1\right)
and
KP\to\infty
.
In particular, when the number of BS antennas is
L=\Theta\left(n\right)
(resp.
L=\Theta\left(\frac{n}{\ln n}\right)
) and the power satisfies
P=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)
(resp.
P=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)
), we can reliably serve
K=\mathcal{O}(n^{2})
users.

E-B

Converse

Assume that
t=\theta K
with
\theta\in S^{\prime}_{\theta}=\left\{\frac{1}{K},\frac{2}{K},\ldots,1\right\}
.
We consider the case where
\epsilon
and
J
are finite positive constants.
Following from Theorem
4
, the minimum required energy-per-bit is larger than
\inf\frac{nP}{J}
, where the infimum is taken over all
P>0
satisfying that

\left(\theta-\epsilon\right)J-h_{2}\left(\epsilon\right)\leq\frac{nL}{K}\log_{2}\left(1+\theta PK\right),\forall\theta\in S^{\prime}_{\theta}.

(185)

When
\theta\leq\theta^{\prime}=\frac{h_{2}\left(\epsilon\right)}{J}+\epsilon
, (
185
) is satisfied for any positive
P,n,L,
and
K
.

Next, assuming
n,K\to\infty
,
KP=\Omega\left(1\right)
, and
J=\Theta(1)
, the inequality in (
185
) holds for any
\theta\in S^{\prime}_{\theta}\cap\left(\theta^{\prime},1\right]
if and only if
\frac{nL\ln PK}{K}=\Omega(1)
, which can be divided into the following two relations:
1)
KP=\Theta\left(1\right)
and
\frac{nL}{K}=\Omega(1)
; 2)
KP\to\infty
and
\frac{nL\ln KP}{K}=\Omega(1)
.
Moreover, since the RHS of (
185
) is a monotonically decreasing function of
K
, when the number of BS antennas is
L=\Theta\left(n\right)
(resp.
L=\Theta\left(\frac{n}{\ln n}\right)
) and the power satisfies
P=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)
(resp.
P=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)
),
K
users can be reliably served only if
K=\mathcal{O}(n^{2})
.

Together with the case of
\theta\leq\theta^{\prime}
, assuming
n,K\to\infty
,
KP=\Omega\left(1\right)
, and
J=\Theta(1)
, the inequality in (
185
) holds for any
\theta\in S^{\prime}_{\theta}
if and only if
\frac{nL\ln PK}{K}=\Omega(1)
, i.e., if and only if one of the two relations mentioned above is satisfied.

Together with the achievability result in Appendix
E-A
, we conclude that assuming
n,K\to\infty
,
\ln K=o(n)
,
KP=\Omega\left(1\right)
, and
J=\Theta(1)
, the PUPE requirement
P_{e}\leq\epsilon
is satisfied if and only if
\frac{nL\ln PK}{K}=\Omega(1)
, i.e., if and only if one of the following two relations is satisfied: 1)
\frac{nL}{K}=\Omega\left(1\right)
and
KP=\Theta\left(1\right)
; 2)
\frac{nL\ln KP}{K}=\Omega\left(1\right)
and
KP\to\infty
.
In particular, when the number of BS antennas is
L=\Theta\left(n\right)
(resp.
L=\Theta\left(\frac{n}{\ln n}\right)
) and the power satisfies
P=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)
(resp.
P=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)
), the number of users that can be reliably served is in the order of
K=\mathcal{O}(n^{2})
.

Appendix F
Proof of Theorem
6

In this appendix, we prove Theorem
6
to establish an achievability bound on the PUPE in the case of no-CSI with known
K_{a}
.
As introduced in Appendix
A
, the PUPE can be upper-bounded by (
102
). The probability
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]
therein, i.e. the probability of the event that there are exactly
t
misdecoded users, is upper-bounded in (
105
) applying Fano’s “good region” technique
[
30
]
.
In the following, we particularize the “good region”-based bound on
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]
given in Appendix
A
to the no-CSI case,
followed by further manipulations on the two probabilities on the RHS of (
105
).

Based on the notation introduced in Appendix
A
, the ML decoding metric
g\left(\mathbf{Y},\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{[{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}_{a}]}\right)
in the case of no-CSI is given by
[
20
]

\displaystyle g\left(\mathbf{Y},\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{[{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}_{a}]}\right)

\displaystyle=L\ln\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+{\sum}_{k\in\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}}\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{(k)}\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{(k)}^{H}\right|+\operatorname{tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+{\sum}_{k\in\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}}\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{(k)}\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{(k)}^{H}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{H}\right)

(186)

\displaystyle=L\ln\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\mathbf{A}{\bm{\Gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}}\mathbf{A}^{H}\right|+\operatorname{tr}\left(\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\mathbf{A}{\bm{\Gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}}\mathbf{A}^{H}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{H}\right).

(187)

Here, the matrix
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times MK}
denotes the concatenation of codebooks of the
K
users, which has i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}\left(0,P^{\prime}\right)
entries;
the matrix
{\bm{\Gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{S}=\operatorname{diag}\left\{{\bm{\gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{S}\right\}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{KM\times KM}
, where
\left[{\bm{\gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{S}\right]_{(k-1)M+W_{k}}=1
if
k\in S
and the
W_{k}
-th codeword is decoded for this user, and
\left[{\bm{\gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{S}\right]_{(k-1)M+W_{k}}=0
otherwise.
Similarly, let
{\bm{\Gamma}}_{S}=\operatorname{diag}\left\{{\bm{\gamma}}_{S}\right\}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{KM\times KM}
be a diagonal matrix, where
\left[{\bm{\gamma}}_{S}\right]_{(k-1)M+W_{k}}=1
if
k\in S
and the
W_{k}
-th codeword is transmitted by this user, and
\left[{\bm{\gamma}}_{S}\right]_{(k-1)M+W_{k}}=0
otherwise.
In the following, we denote
g\left(\mathbf{Y},\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{[{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}_{a}]}\right)
as
g\left({\bm{\Gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}}\right)
for simplicity.

Let
\mathbf{A}_{S}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times|S|}
denote the concatenation of transmitted codewords of active users in the set
S\subset\mathcal{K}_{a}
and
let
\mathbf{A}^{{}^{\prime}}_{S_{2}}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times|S_{2}|}
denote the concatenation of false-alarm codewords for users in the set
S_{2}\subset\mathcal{K}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{a}\cup S_{1}
.
Denote
{\mathbf{A}}_{all}=\left\{{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}\backslash S_{1}},{\mathbf{A}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{S_{2}}\right\}
.
Define
\mathbf{F}
,
\mathbf{F}^{{}^{\prime}}
, and
\mathbf{F}_{1}
as in (
41
), (
42
), and (
43
), respectively.
The conditional expectation in (
107
) can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},\mathbf{Z}}\left[\left.\exp\left\{(u-r)g\left(\bm{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}\right)-ug\left({\bm{\Gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}\backslash S_{1}\cup S_{2}}\right)+r\omega g\left({\bm{\Gamma}}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}\backslash S_{1}}\right)\right\}\right|\mathbf{c}_{[{\mathcal{K}}_{a}]},\mathbf{c}_{[{\mathcal{K}}_{a}\backslash S_{1}]},\mathbf{c}^{{}^{\prime}}_{[S_{2}]}\right]

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{(u-r)L\ln\left|\mathbf{F}\right|-uL\ln\left|\mathbf{F}^{{}^{\prime}}\right|+r\omega L\ln\left|\mathbf{F}_{1}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},\mathbf{Z}}\left[\left.\exp\left\{\operatorname{tr}\left(\!\mathbf{Y}^{H}\!\left((u-r)\mathbf{F}^{-1}-u\left(\mathbf{F}^{{}^{\prime}}\right)^{-1}+r\omega\mathbf{F}_{1}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{Y}\right)\right\}\right|\mathbf{A}_{all}\right]

(188)

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{L\left((u-r)\ln\left|\mathbf{F}\right|-u\ln\left|{\mathbf{F}^{{}^{\prime}}}\right|+r\omega\ln\left|\mathbf{F}_{1}\right|-\ln\left|\mathbf{B}\right|\right)\right\}.

(189)

Here, (
189
) follows from Lemma
15
by taking the expectation over
\mathbf{H}
and
\mathbf{Z}
provided that the minimum eigenvalue of
\mathbf{B}
satisfies
\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{B}\right)>0
, where the matrix
\mathbf{B}
is given by

\mathbf{B}=(1-u+r)\mathbf{I}_{n}+u\left(\mathbf{F}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{F}-r\omega\mathbf{F}_{1}^{-1}\mathbf{F}.

(190)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\!\left[\bigcup_{S_{1}}\bigcup_{S_{2}}\bigcup_{\mathbf{c}^{{}^{\prime}}_{[S_{2}]}}\left\{g\left({\bm{\Gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}\backslash S_{1}\cup S_{2}}\right)\leq g\left(\bm{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}\right)\right\}\bigcap\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{t}\;\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{all}}\!\!\left[\min_{{\begin{subarray}{c}u\geq 0,r\geq 0,\\
\lambda_{\min}\left({{\mathbf{B}}}\right)>0\end{subarray}}}\!\exp\!\left\{L\!\left(rn\nu+(u-r)\ln\left|\mathbf{F}\right|-u\ln\left|{\mathbf{F}^{{}^{\prime}}}\right|+r\omega\ln\left|\mathbf{F}_{1}\right|-\ln\left|\mathbf{B}\right|\right)\right\}\right]\!,

(191)

where
C_{t}={\binom{K_{a}}{t}}{\binom{K-K_{a}+t}{t}}M^{t}
.
Here, (
191
) follows by substituting (
189
) into (
107
) and follows from the fact that the expectation in (
191
) is unchanged for different
S_{1}
,
S_{2}
, and
\mathbf{c}^{{}^{\prime}}_{[S_{2}]}
once
t
is fixed, considering that the codebook matrix
\mathbf{A}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,P^{\prime})
entries.
As a result, the first probability on the RHS of (
105
) is upper-bounded by (
191
), denoted as
q_{1,t}\left(\omega,\nu\right)
in (
39
).

Next, we proceed to upper-bound the second term
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}^{c}\right]
on the RHS of (
105
).
Denote
\mathbf{A}_{all}=\left\{\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}},\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}\backslash S_{1}}\right\}
and define the event
\mathcal{G}_{\delta}\!=\!\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\!\!\frac{\chi_{i}^{2}(2L)}{2}\!\leq\!nL(1+\delta)\right\}
for
\delta\geq 0
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\!\left[\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcup_{S_{1}}\left\{g\left(\bm{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}\right)>\omega g\left({\bm{\Gamma}}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}\backslash S_{1}}\right)+nL\nu\right\}\right]

(192)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{all}}\!\left[\mathbb{P}\left[\left.\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}^{H}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\omega\mathbf{F}^{\frac{H}{2}}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{-1}\mathbf{F}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}\right)>C_{F}\right|\mathbf{A}_{all}\right]\right]

(193)

\displaystyle\leq\!\min_{\delta\geq 0}\sum_{S_{1}}\!\!\left\{\!\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{all}}\!\!\left[\mathbb{P}\!\left[\left.\!\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\!\left(1-\omega-\omega\lambda_{i}\right)\frac{\chi_{i}^{2}(2L)}{2}\!>C_{F}\!\right\}\bigcap\mathcal{G}_{\delta}\right|\mathbf{A}_{all}\right]\right]\!+\!\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\delta}^{c}\right]\right\}

(194)

\displaystyle=\min_{\delta\geq 0}\left\{\sum_{S_{1}}q_{3,t}\left(\omega,\nu\right)+\binom{K_{a}}{t}\left(1-\frac{\gamma\left(nL,nL\left(1+\delta\right)\right)}{\Gamma\left(nL\right)}\right)\right\},

(195)

where
C_{F}\!=\!\omega L\ln\left|\mathbf{F}_{1}\right|-L\ln\left|\mathbf{F}\right|+nL\nu
;
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n}
are eigenvalues of
\mathbf{F}_{1}^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}^{H}
in decreasing order with the first
m=\min\left\{n,t\right\}
eigenvalues being positive and all of the rest being
0
.
Here, (
193
) follows from the union bound and the fact that
\mathbf{y}_{l}=\mathbf{F}^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{CN}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{F}\right)
conditioned on
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}
, where
\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{CN}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{n}\right)
for
l\in[L]
;
(
194
) follows from the bounding technique in (
13
), and the fact that
conditioned on
\mathbf{A}_{all}
,

\mathcal{U}\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}
have the same distribution as
\mathcal{CN}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{n}\right)
for the unitary matrix
\mathcal{U}
satisfying
\mathcal{U}^{H}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\omega\mathbf{F}^{\frac{H}{2}}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{-1}\mathbf{F}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\mathcal{U}=\bm{\Lambda}
,
where
\bm{\Lambda}=\operatorname{diag}\left\{1-\omega-\omega\lambda_{1},\ldots,1-\omega-\omega\lambda_{n}\right\}
;
(
195
) holds because
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\chi_{i}^{2}(2L)
has the same distribution as
\chi^{2}(2nL)
considering that
\chi_{i}^{2}(2L)
,
i=1,\ldots,n
, are independent.

The first term on the RHS of (
195
) can be bounded as

\displaystyle\sum_{S_{1}}q_{3,t}\left(\omega,\nu\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{all}}\!\left[\mathbb{P}\left[\left.\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{i}\frac{\chi_{i}^{2}(2L)}{2}<\frac{nL(1+\delta)(1-\omega)-C_{F}}{\omega}\right|\mathbf{A}_{all}\right]\right]

(196)

\displaystyle\leq\binom{K_{a}}{t}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{all}}\!\left[\frac{\gamma\left(Lm,L\prod_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}^{-\frac{1}{m}}\frac{n(1+\delta)(1-\omega)-\omega\ln\left|\mathbf{F}_{1}\right|+\ln\left|\mathbf{F}\right|-n\nu}{\omega}\right)}{\Gamma\left(Lm\right)}\right],

(197)

where (
197
) follows from Lemma
16
and the fact that the number of non-zero eigenvalues of
\mathbf{F}_{1}^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}\mathbf{A}_{S_{1}}^{H}
is
m=\min\left\{n,t\right\}
, which are denoted as
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{m}
in decreasing order as aforementioned.
Substituting (
197
) into (
195
), we can obtain an upper bound on
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}^{c}\right]
, which is denoted as
q_{2,t}\left(\omega,\nu\right)
in (
44
).

In conclusion, based on Fano’s bounding technique, we have obtained
q_{1,t}\left(\omega,\nu\right)
in (
39
) (i.e. an upper bound on the first probability in (
105
)) and
q_{2,t}\left(\omega,\nu\right)
in (
44
) (i.e. an upper bound on the second probability in (
105
)),
which contributes to an upper bound
p_{t}
given in (
38
) on the probability
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]
.
This completes the proof of Theorem
6
.

Appendix G
Proof of Theorem
8

In this appendix, we prove Theorem
8
to establish an achievability bound on the PUPE for the scenario in which the number
K_{a}
of active users is random and unknown.
In this case, the decoder first obtains an estimate
{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}
of
K_{a}
via an energy-based estimator.
Then, given
{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}
, the decoder produces a set of decoded codewords, which is denoted as
\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{[\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}]}
.
The number of codewords in the set
\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{[\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}]}
belongs to an interval around
{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}
,
i.e., it is satisfied that
|\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}_{a,l}^{{}^{\prime}},{\rm{K}}_{a,u}^{{}^{\prime}}]
,
where
{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l}=\max\left\{0,{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}-r^{\prime}\right\}
,
{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}=\min\left\{K,{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}+r^{\prime}\right\}
, and
r^{\prime}
denotes a nonnegative integer referred to as the decoding radius.
Based on the notation introduced in Appendix
A
, the per-user probability of misdetection in (
9
) can be upper-bounded as

\displaystyle P_{e,\mathrm{MD}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{K_{a}}\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{K}_{a}}}1\left[W_{k}\neq\hat{W}_{k}\right]\right]

(198)

\displaystyle\leq\!\sum_{{\rm{K}}_{a}=1}^{K}\!\!P_{K_{a}}({\rm{K}}_{a})\!\sum_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}=0}^{K}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}}}\!\frac{t+({\rm{K}}_{a}-{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u})^{+}}{{\rm{K}}_{a}}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\cap\left\{{\rm{K}}_{a}\!\to\!{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right\}\right]_{\text{no power constraint}}+p_{0}.

(199)

Here, the integer
t
takes value in
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}}
defined in (
51
) because the number of misdetected codewords, given by
t+({\rm{K}}_{a}-{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u})^{+}
, is lower-bounded by
({\rm{K}}_{a}-{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u})^{+}
and upper-bounded by the total number
{\rm{K}}_{a}
of transmitted messages;

\mathcal{F}_{t}
denotes the event that there are exactly
t+({\rm{K}}_{a}-{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u})^{+}
misdetected codewords;

\left\{{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\right\}
denotes the event that the estimation of
K_{a}
results in
{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}
;

p_{0}
denotes an upper bound on the total variation distance between the measures with and without power constraint given by

p_{0}=\mathbb{E}[K_{a}]\left(1-\frac{\gamma\left(n,\frac{nP}{{P}^{\prime}}\right)}{\Gamma\left(n\right)}\right).

(200)

Likewise, the per-user probability of false-alarm in (
10
) can be upper-bounded as

\displaystyle P_{e,\mathrm{FA}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|}\sum_{k\in{\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}}}1\left[\hat{W}_{k}\neq{W}_{k}\right]\right]

(201)

\displaystyle\leq\!\sum_{{\rm{K}}_{a}=0}^{K}\!P_{K_{a}}({\rm{K}}_{a})\!\!\sum_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}=0}^{K}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}}}\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},t}}\!\!\!\frac{t^{\prime}+({\rm{K}}_{a,l}^{{}^{\prime}}\!-\!{\rm{K}}_{a})^{+}}{\hat{\rm{K}}_{a}}\mathbb{P}\!\left[\mathcal{F}_{t,t^{\prime}}\!\cap\!\left\{{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right\}\right]_{\text{no power constraint}}+p_{0},

(202)

where
\hat{\rm{K}}_{a}
denotes the number of detected codewords as given in (
54
);

\mathcal{F}_{t,t^{\prime}}
denotes the event that there are exactly
t+({\rm{K}}_{a}-{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u})^{+}
misdetected codewords and
t^{\prime}+({\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l}-{\rm{K}}_{a})^{+}
falsely alarmed codewords;
the integer
t^{\prime}
takes value in
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}}
defined in (
53
) because:
i)
\hat{\rm{K}}_{a}
must be in
[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l}:{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]
;
ii) the number of falsely alarmed codewords is lower-bounded by
({\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l}-{\rm{K}}_{a})^{+}
;
iii) there exist falsely alarmed codewords only when
\hat{\rm{K}}_{a}\geq 1
.

Next, we omit the subscript “no power constraint” for the sake of brevity.
The probability in the RHS of
199
can be bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\cap\left\{{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\cap\left\{|\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]\right\}\cap\left\{{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right\}\right]

(203)

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t}\cap\left\{|\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]\right\}\right],\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right]\right\}

(204)

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},t}}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t,t^{\prime}}\left||\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]\right.\right],\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right]\right\}.

(205)

Here,
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},t}
is defined in (
52
), which is obtained similar to
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},t}
with the difference that the number
\hat{\rm{K}}_{a}
of detected codewords can be
0
;
(
203
) follows because the event
{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}
implies that
|\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]

[
19
]
;
(
204
) follows from the fact that the joint probability is upper-bounded by each of the individual probabilities.
Similarly, the probability in the RHS of
202
can be bounded as

\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t,t^{\prime}}\cap\left\{{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right\}\right]\leq\min\left\{\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t,t^{\prime}}\left||\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]\right.\right],\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right]\right\}.

(206)

Then, we proceed to bound
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t,t^{\prime}}\left||\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]\right.\right]
and
\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right]
, respectively,
which are two ingredients of upper-bounding
P_{e,\mathrm{MD}}
and
P_{e,\mathrm{FA}}
.

G-A

Upper-bounding
\mathbb{P}\!\left[{\rm{K}}_{a}\!\to\!{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right]

Given the channel output
\mathbf{Y}
, the receiver estimates
K_{a}
as

{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}=\arg\min_{\tilde{{\rm{K}}}_{a}\in\left[{\rm{K}}_{l},{\rm{K}}_{u}\right]}m(\mathbf{Y},\tilde{{\rm{K}}}_{a}),

(207)

where
m(\mathbf{Y},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a})
denotes the energy-based estimation metric given by

m\left(\mathbf{Y},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}\right)=\left|\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{F}^{2}-nL\left(1+\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}P^{\prime}\right)\right|.

(208)

Denote
C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}=\frac{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}+\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}{2}
.
In the case of
\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}\neq{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}
, the event
m\left(\mathbf{Y},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right)\leq m(\mathbf{Y},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a})
is equivalent to

\begin{cases}\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq nL\left(1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right),&\text{ if }{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}<\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}\\
\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{F}^{2}\geq nL\left(1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right),&\text{ if }{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}>\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}\end{cases}.

(209)

As a result, the probability of the event that
K_{a}
is estimated as
{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}
is upper-bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm{K}}_{a}\!\to\!{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right]\!

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[m\left(\mathbf{Y},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right)\leq m(\mathbf{Y},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}),\forall\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}\neq{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right]

(210)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}\in\left[0:K\right],\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}\neq{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}}\mathbb{P}\left[m\left(\mathbf{Y},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\right)\leq m(\mathbf{Y},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a})\right]

(211)

\displaystyle=\min_{\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}\in\left[0:K\right],\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}\neq{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}}1\!\left[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\!<\!\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}\right]\mathbb{P}\!\left[\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq nL\!\left(\!1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;+1\!\left[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a}\!>\!\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}\right]\mathbb{P}\!\left[\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{F}^{2}\geq nL\!\left(\!1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right)\right].

(212)

The probability
\mathbb{P}\!\left[\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq nL\left(1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right)\right]
on the RHS of (
212
) can be bounded following two approaches.
First, applying the Chernoff bound and Lemma
15
, we have

\mathbb{P}\!\left[\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq nL\left(1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}}\!\!\left[\min_{\rho\geq 0}\exp\left\{\rho nL\left(1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right)-L\ln\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{F}\right|\right\}\right],

(213)

where
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times{\rm{K}}_{a}}
denotes the concatenation of the transmitted codewords of
{\rm{K}}_{a}
active users and
\mathbf{F}=\mathbf{I}_{n}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{H}
.
Define the event
\mathcal{G}_{\eta}=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\chi_{i}^{2}(2L)}{2}\geq nL\eta\right\}
for
\eta\geq 0
.
Then, we can obtain another upper bound as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq nL\left(1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}}\!\left[\mathbb{P}\left[\left.\sum_{l=1}^{L}\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}^{H}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{H}\right)\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}\leq nL\left(1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right)\right|\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}\right]\right]

(214)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{\eta>0}\!\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}}\!\!\left[\mathbb{P}\!\left[\left.\!\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1+\lambda^{\prime}_{i})\frac{\chi^{2}_{i}(2L)}{2}\!\leq\!nL\!\left(1\!+\!C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right)\right\}\cap\mathcal{G}_{\eta}\right|\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}\right]\right]\!+\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\eta}^{c}\right]\right\}

(215)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{\eta>0}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}}\!\!\left[\mathbb{P}\left[\left.\prod_{i=1}^{m^{\prime}}{(\lambda^{{}^{\prime}}_{i})}^{\frac{1}{m^{\prime}}}\frac{\chi^{2}(2Lm^{\prime})}{2}\leq nL\left(1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}-\eta\right)\right|\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}\right]\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\eta}^{c}\right]\right\}

(216)

\displaystyle=\min_{\eta>0}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}}\!\!\left[\frac{\gamma\left(Lm^{\prime},\prod_{i=1}^{m^{\prime}}{(\lambda^{{}^{\prime}}_{i})}^{-\frac{1}{m^{\prime}}}nL\left(1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}-\eta\right)\right)}{\Gamma\left(Lm^{\prime}\right)}\right]+\frac{\gamma\left(nL,nL\eta\right)}{\Gamma\left(nL\right)}\right\},

(217)

where
\lambda^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\lambda^{\prime}_{n}
are eigenvalues of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}^{H}
in decreasing order with the first
m^{\prime}=\min\left\{n,{\rm{K}}_{a}\right\}
eigenvalues being positive and others being
0
.
Here, (
214
) holds because
\mathbf{y}_{l}=\mathbf{F}^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}\sim\mathcal{CN}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{F}\right)
conditioned on
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}
, where
\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{l}\sim\mathcal{CN}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{n}\right)
;
(
215
) follows from the “good region” technique in (
13
);
(
216
) follows from Lemma
16
.
Taking the minimum of (
213
) and (
217
), we obtain the ultimate upper bound on
\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq nL\left(1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right)\right]
denoted as
p_{{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},1}
in (
8
).

Likewise, we can derive two upper bounds on
\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{F}^{2}\geq nL\left(1+C_{{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},\tilde{\rm{K}}_{a}}P^{\prime}\right)\right]
.
Taking the minimum value of them, we obtain the ultimate upper bound on it, denoted as
p_{{\rm{K}}_{a}\to{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a},2}
in (
8
).

G-B

Upper-bounding
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t,t^{\prime}}\left||\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]\right.\right]

In this subsection, we utilize the MAP decoder to upper-bound
\mathbb{P}\!\left[\mathcal{F}_{t,t^{\prime}}\left||\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]\right.\right]
.
Under the condition that
|\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]
, the outputs of the decoder are given by

\left[\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a},\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{[\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}]}\right]=\arg\min_{{\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}\subset\mathcal{K}},{|\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]}}\;\min_{\left(\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{(k)}\in\mathcal{C}_{k}\right)_{k\in\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}}}\;g\left(\mathbf{Y},\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{[\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}]}\right),

(218)

\hat{W}_{k}=\emph{f}_{\text{en},k}^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{(k)}\right),\;\;k\in\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a},

(219)

where the MAP decoding metric
g\left(\mathbf{Y},\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{[{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}_{a}]}\right)
is given by

g\!\left(\mathbf{Y},\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{[{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}_{a}]}\right)=L\ln\!\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}\!+\!\mathbf{A}{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\prime}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}}\mathbf{A}^{H}\right|+\operatorname{tr}\!\left(\!\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}\!+\!\mathbf{A}{\bm{\Gamma}}^{\prime}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}}\mathbf{A}^{H}\right)^{-1}\!\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{H}\right)-\ln\!\left(P_{K_{a}}(|{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}_{a}|)M^{-|{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}_{a}|}\right).

(220)

Here,
{\bm{\Gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{S}
is defined in Appendix
F
.
In the following,
g\left(\mathbf{Y},\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{[{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}_{a}]}\right)
is denoted as
g\left(\hat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}}\right)
for simplicity.

Let the set
S_{1}\subset\mathcal{K}_{a}
of size
t+({\rm{K}}_{a}-{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u})^{+}
denote the set of users whose codewords are misdecoded.
The set
S_{1}
can be divided into two subsets
S_{1,1}
and
S_{1,2}
of size
({\rm{K}}_{a}-{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u})^{+}
and
t
, respectively.
Let the set
S_{2}\subset\mathcal{K}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{a}\cup S_{1}
of size
t^{\prime}+({\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l}-{\rm{K}}_{a})^{+}
denote the set of detected users with false-alarm codewords.
Let
S_{2,1}
denote an arbitrary subset of
S_{2}
of size
({\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l}-{\rm{K}}_{a})^{+}
.
For the sake of simplicity, we rewrite “
\bigcup_{{S_{1}\subset\mathcal{K}_{a},}{\left|S_{1}\right|=t+({\rm{K}}_{a}-{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u})^{+}}}
” to “
\bigcup_{S_{1}}
”
and “
\bigcup_{{S_{2}\subset\mathcal{K}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{a}\cup S_{1},\left|S_{2}\right|=t^{\prime}+({\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l}-{\rm{K}}_{a})^{+}}}
” to “
\bigcup_{S_{2}}
”; similarly for
\sum
and
\bigcap
.
We rewrite
\left\{\mathbf{c}^{\prime}_{(k)}\in\mathcal{C}_{k}:k\in S_{2},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}_{(k)}\neq\mathbf{c}_{(k)}\right\}
to
\mathbf{c}^{{}^{\prime}}_{[S_{2}]}
for short, which denotes the set of false alarm codewords corresponding to users in the set
S_{2}
.
Define
\mathbf{A}_{S}
,
\mathbf{A}^{{}^{\prime}}_{S}
,
{\bm{\Gamma}}_{S}
and
{\bm{\Gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{S}
as in Appendix
F
.
Define the event
\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}=\bigcap_{S_{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Y}\in\mathcal{R}_{t,S_{1}}\right\}
as in Appendix
A
.
Following similar ideas in (
105
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{F}_{t,t^{\prime}}\left||\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]\right.\right]

\displaystyle\leq\!\min_{0\leq\omega\leq 1,\nu\geq 0}\!\left\{\!\mathbb{P}\!\left[\left.{\bigcup}_{S_{1}}{\bigcup}_{S_{2}}{\bigcup}_{\mathbf{c}^{{}^{\prime}}_{[S_{2}]}}\!\!\left\{g\!\left({\bm{\Gamma}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}\backslash S_{1}\cup S_{2}}\right)\leq g\!\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{{}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}\backslash S_{1,1}\cup S_{2,1}}\right)\right\}\cap\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}\right|\!|\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\!\in\![{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]\right]\right.

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\left.\!+\mathbb{P}\!\left[\left.\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}^{c}\right||\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{a}|\in[{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,l},{\rm{K}}^{\prime}_{a,u}]\right]\right\}.

(221)

Similar to (
107
) and (
191
), we can obtain an upper bound on the first probability on the RHS of (
221
),
which is denoted as
q_{1,{\rm K}^{\prime}_{a},t,t^{\prime}}
in (
58
).
In the case of
t+({\rm K}_{a}-{\rm K}^{\prime}_{a,u})^{+}>0
, the second probability on the RHS of (
221
) can be bounded as in Appendix
F
.
When
t+({\rm K}_{a}-{\rm K}^{\prime}_{a,u})^{+}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{G}_{\omega,\nu}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[g\left(\bm{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}\right)>\frac{nL\nu}{1-\omega}\right]

(222)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{K}_{a}}}\left[1-\frac{\gamma\left(nL,\frac{nL\nu}{1-\omega}-L\ln\left|\mathbf{F}\right|+b\right)}{\Gamma\left(nL\right)}\right],

(223)

where the constant
b
is given in (
63
).
The RHS of (
223
) is denoted as
q_{2,{\rm K}^{\prime}_{a},t,0}
in (
68
).
This concludes the proof of Theorem
8
.

Appendix H
Proof of Theorem
9

In this appendix, we prove Theorem
9
to establish a Fano type converse bound on the minimum required energy-per-bit for the no-CSI case.
Let
\bar{\mathbf{y}}=\left[\mathbf{y}_{1}^{T},\mathbf{y}_{2}^{T},\ldots,\mathbf{y}_{L}^{T}\right]^{T}\in\mathbb{C}^{nL\times 1}
be a vector obtained by concatenating the received signals of
L
antennas at the BS.
Let
\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}
be an
n\times K_{a}M
submatrix of
{\mathbf{X}}
including codebooks of
K_{a}
active users and denote
\bar{\mathbf{X}}=\operatorname{diag}\left\{\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M},\ldots,\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}\right\}\in\mathbb{C}^{nL\times K_{a}ML}
.
Let
\bar{\mathbf{H}}_{l}\in\mathbb{C}^{K_{a}M\times K_{a}M}
be a block diagonal matrix, where block
k
is a diagonal
M\times M
matrix with all diagonal entries equal to
h_{k,l}\sim\mathcal{CN}(0,1)
.
Let
\bar{\mathbf{H}}=\left[\bar{\mathbf{H}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\mathbf{H}}_{L}\right]^{T}\in\mathbb{C}^{K_{a}ML\times K_{a}M}
.
The vector
\bar{\bm{\beta}}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{K_{a}M}
includes
K_{a}
blocks, where each block is of size
M
and includes one 1; we have
\left[\bar{\bm{\beta}}\right]_{(k-1)M+W_{k}}=1
if the
W_{k}
-th codeword is transmitted by user
k
, and
\left[\bar{\bm{\beta}}\right]_{(k-1)M+W_{k}}=0
otherwise.
Then, we can model the communication system as

\bar{\mathbf{y}}=\bar{\mathbf{X}}\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}}+\bar{\mathbf{z}},

(224)

where
\bar{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathbb{C}^{nL\times 1}
with each entry i.i.d. from
\mathcal{CN}(0,1)
.

We assume a genie reveals the set of active users.
Similar to the analysis in Appendix
D
, we have
[
8
]

\left(1-\epsilon\right)J-h_{2}\left(\epsilon\right)\leq\frac{1}{K_{a}}I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right).

(225)

Based on the chain rule of the mutual information, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\bm{\beta}},\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)

\displaystyle=I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)+I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\bm{\beta}},\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)

(226)

\displaystyle=I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)+I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}},\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right).

(227)

Since
\bar{\bm{\beta}}\to\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}}\to(\bar{\mathbf{y}},\bar{\mathbf{X}})
forms a Markov chain, the mutual information
I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}},\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)=0
. Hence, we have
[
40
, Eq. (78)]

I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)=I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)-I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\bm{\beta}},\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right).

(228)

Next, we focus on the two terms on the RHS of (
228
).
We have

\displaystyle I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{X}}=\bar{\mathbf{X}}^{r}\right)

\displaystyle=I_{2}\left(\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{X}}^{r}\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}}+\bar{\mathbf{z}}\right)

(229)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbf{u}}I_{2}\left(\mathbf{u};\bar{\mathbf{X}}^{r}\mathbf{u}+\bar{\mathbf{z}}\right)

(230)

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{nL}+\frac{1}{M}\bar{\mathbf{X}}^{r}\left(\bar{\mathbf{X}}^{r}\right)^{H}\right|

(231)

\displaystyle=L\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{1}{M}\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}^{r}\left(\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}^{r}\right)^{H}\right|,

(232)

where
\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}^{r}
is a realization of
\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}
and
\bar{\mathbf{X}}^{r}\!=\!\operatorname{diag}\!\left\{\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}^{r},\ldots,\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}^{r}\right\}
is a realization of
\bar{\mathbf{X}}
.
The supremum in (
230
) is over

\mathbf{u}
with
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathbf{u}\right]\!=\!\mathbf{0}
and
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{H}\right]\!=\!\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left(\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}}\right)\!\left(\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}}\right)^{H}\right]\!=\!\frac{1}{M}\mathbf{I}_{K_{a}ML}
.
The supremum is achieved when
\mathbf{u}\!\sim\!\mathcal{CN}\!\left(\mathbf{0},\frac{1}{M}\mathbf{I}_{K_{a}ML}\right)

[
40
]
, which implies (
231
).
Then, we have

I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)\leq L\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{1}{M}\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}^{H}\right|\right].

(233)

Under the assumption that the entries of codebooks are i.i.d. with mean zero and variance
P
, the expectation on the RHS of (
233
) can be upper-bounded as

\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{1}{M}\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}M}^{H}\right|\right]\leq\min\left\{n\log_{2}\left(1+K_{a}P\right),K_{a}M\log_{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{M}nP\right)\right\},

(234)

where (
234
) follows from the concavity of the
\log_{2}\left|\cdot\right|
function.
We denote the RHS of (
234
) as
C
for simplicity.

A lower bound on
I\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\bm{\beta}},\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)
can be derived as follows. Let
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times K_{a}}
be a submatrix of
{\mathbf{X}}
formed by columns corresponding to the support of
\bar{\bm{\beta}}
.
Let
\tilde{\mathbf{H}}_{K_{a}}
be a
K_{a}\times L
submatrix of
{\mathbf{H}}
including fading coefficients between
K_{a}
active users and
L
antennas of the receiver.
Then, the received signal given in (
5
) can be rewritten as

\mathbf{Y}=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}\tilde{\mathbf{H}}_{K_{a}}+{\mathbf{Z}}.

(235)

We have

\displaystyle I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\bm{\beta}}=\bar{\bm{\beta}}^{r},\bar{\mathbf{X}}=\bar{\mathbf{X}}^{r}\right)

\displaystyle=I_{2}\left(\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}}^{r};\bar{\mathbf{X}}^{r}\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}}^{r}+\bar{\mathbf{z}}\right)

(236)

\displaystyle=I_{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{H}}_{K_{a}};\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}^{r}\tilde{\mathbf{H}}_{K_{a}}+{\mathbf{Z}}\right)

(237)

\displaystyle=L\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}^{r}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}^{r}\right)^{H}\right|,

(238)

where
\bar{\bm{\beta}}^{r}
is a realization of
\bar{\bm{\beta}}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}^{r}
is a realization of
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}
. Hence, applying Sylvester’s determinant theorem, we have

I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\bm{\beta}},\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)=L\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}^{H}\right|\right]=L\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{K_{a}}+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}^{H}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}\right|\right].

(239)

Substituting (
234
) and (
239
) into (
228
), we obtain an upper bound on
I\left(\left.\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)
.
Substituting this bound into (
225
), the proof of (
73
) in Theorem
9
is completed.

Under the assumption that
K
users generate their codebooks independently with each entry i.i.d. from
\mathcal{CN}(0,P)
, we further lower-bound
\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{K_{a}}+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}^{H}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}\right|\right]
in (
239
) in the remainder of this appendix.
In the case of
K_{a}>n
, let
\alpha_{i}\sim\frac{\chi^{2}\left(2\left(K_{a}-i+1\right)\right)}{2}
for
i=1,\ldots,n
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{K_{a}}+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}^{H}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\left(1+P\alpha_{i}\right)\right]

(240)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\alpha_{i}\right]+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\log_{2}\!\left({P+\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}}}\right)\right]

(241)

\displaystyle\geq\log_{2}e\!\sum_{i=1}^{n}\!\psi(K_{a}-i+1)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log_{2}\!\left({P+\frac{1}{K_{a}-i+1}}\right),

(242)

where (
240
) follows from Lemma
18
shown below;
(
242
) follows because
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\ln\frac{\chi^{2}\left(2b\right)}{2}\right]\!=\!\psi(b)
with
\psi(x)
denoting Euler’s digamma function, and follows from Jensen’s inequality considering
\log_{2}\left(P+\frac{1}{x}\right)
is a convex function of
x
.
Let
b_{1}=J{\left(1-\epsilon\right)}-h_{2}\left(\epsilon\right)
.
Substituting (
242
) into (
73
), we have

b_{1}\leq\frac{LC}{K_{a}}-\frac{L}{K_{a}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\psi(K_{a}-i+1)\log_{2}e+\log_{2}\!\left({P+\frac{1}{K_{a}-i+1}}\right)\right).

(243)

Lemma 18
(Section 4.1.1 in
[
49
]
)

For
b>0
. A central complex Wishart matrix
\mathbf{W}\sim\mathcal{W}_{m}(n,\mathbf{I})
, with
n\geq m
, satisfies

\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{m}+b\mathbf{W}\right|\right]>\sum_{i=n-m+1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\left(1+b\frac{\chi^{2}(2i)}{2}\right)\right],

(244)

where
\chi^{2}(2i)
is a chi-square variate with
2i
degrees of freedom.

Likewise, when
K_{a}\leq n
, we have

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{K_{a}}+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}^{H}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}\right|\right]\geq\log_{2}e\sum_{i=1}^{K_{a}}\psi(n-i+1)+\sum_{i=1}^{K_{a}}\log_{2}\!\left({P+\frac{1}{n-i+1}}\right).

(245)

Substituting (
245
) into (
73
), when
K_{a}\leq n
, we have

b_{1}\leq\frac{LC}{K_{a}}-\frac{L}{K_{a}}\sum_{i=1}^{K_{a}}\left(\psi(n-i+1)\log_{2}e+\log_{2}\!\left({P+\frac{1}{n-i+1}}\right)\right).

(246)

Together with (
243
), the proof of (
75
) is completed, which concludes the proof of Theorem
9
.

Appendix I
Proof of Theorem
10

In this appendix, we prove Theorem
10
to establish a converse bound on the minimum required energy-per-bit for the case in which there is no CSI at the receiver and the number
K_{a}
of active users is random and unknown.
In Appendix
I-A
, we establish a converse bound for the scenario with multiple users;
in Appendix
I-B
, we establish a converse bound for the scenario with knowledge of the activities of
K-1
potential users and the transmitted codewords and channel coefficients of active users among them, which is also a converse bound for the massive random access problem.

I-A

Multiple-user random access converse bound

In this part, we use the Fano inequality to derive a converse bound on the minimum required energy-per-bit for the multiple-user case when
K_{a}
is random and unknown.
Define
\bar{\mathbf{y}}
,
\bar{\mathbf{X}}
,
\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{KM}
,
\bar{\mathbf{H}}_{l}
, and
\bar{\mathbf{H}}
as in Appendix
H
.
Let the vector
\bar{\bm{\beta}}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{KM}
indicate which codewords are transmitted by active users, which includes
K
blocks with each block of size
M
and including at most one 1.
Specifically, according to the random access model described in Section
II
, for
m\in[M]
and
k\in[K]
, we have
\mathbb{P}\left[\left[\bar{\bm{\beta}}\right]_{(k-1)M+m}=0\right]=1-\frac{p_{a}}{M}
and
\mathbb{P}\left[\left[\bar{\bm{\beta}}\right]_{(k-1)M+m}=1\right]=\frac{p_{a}}{M}
.
Then, we can model the communication system as

\bar{\mathbf{y}}=\bar{\mathbf{X}}\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}}+\bar{\mathbf{z}},

(247)

where
\bar{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathbb{C}^{nL\times 1}
with each entry i.i.d. from
\mathcal{CN}(0,1)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{k}=1\left[{W}_{k}\neq\hat{W}_{k}\right]
and
P_{e,k}=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{M}_{k}\right]
.
The error requirements in (
9
) and (
10
) can be loosened to

P_{e}=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}}P_{e,k}\leq\epsilon_{\rm MD}+\epsilon_{\rm FA}.

(248)

For
k\in\mathcal{K}
, a Fano type argument gives

(1-P_{e,k})H_{2}(W_{k}|\bar{\mathbf{X}})-h_{2}(P_{e,k})\leq I_{2}(W_{k};\hat{W}_{k}|\bar{\mathbf{X}}),

(249)

where
H_{2}(x)
denotes the entropy of a random variable
x
and
h_{2}(\cdot)
denotes the binary entropy function.
The entropy
H_{2}(W_{k}|\bar{\mathbf{X}})
can be computed as

H_{2}(W_{k}|\bar{\mathbf{X}})=H_{2}(W_{k})=-(1-p_{a})\log_{2}(1-p_{a})-p_{a}\log_{2}\frac{p_{a}}{M}=h_{2}(p_{a})+p_{a}J.

(250)

Substituting (
250
) into (
249
) and taking the summation over
k\in\mathcal{K}
on both sides of (
249
), we have

K(1-P_{e})\;\!(h_{2}(p_{a})+p_{a}J)-\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}}h_{2}(P_{e,k})\leq\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}}I_{2}(W_{k};\hat{W}_{k}\;\!|\;\!\bar{\mathbf{X}}).

(251)

Considering the concavity of
h_{2}(\cdot)
and the inequality that
P_{e}\leq\epsilon_{\rm MD}+\epsilon_{\rm FA}\leq 1-\frac{1}{1+2^{h_{2}(p_{a})+p_{a}J}}
, we have

P_{e}\;\!(h_{2}(p_{a})+p_{a}J)+\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}}h_{2}(P_{e,k})\leq(\epsilon_{\rm MD}+\epsilon_{\rm FA})\;\!(h_{2}(p_{a})+p_{a}J)+h_{2}(\epsilon_{\rm MD}+\epsilon_{\rm FA}).

(252)

Moreover, following from (
168
), we have
\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}}I_{2}(W_{k};\hat{W}_{k}\;\!\!|\;\!\!\bar{\mathbf{X}})\leq I_{2}(W_{\mathcal{K}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\;\!\!|\;\!\!\bar{\mathbf{X}})=I_{2}(\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\;\!\!|\;\!\!\bar{\mathbf{X}})
.
Together with (
228
), (
251
), and (
252
), we can obtain

K(1-\epsilon_{\rm MD}-\epsilon_{\rm FA})\left(h_{2}(p_{a})+p_{a}J\right)-Kh_{2}(\epsilon_{\rm MD}+\epsilon_{\rm FA})\leq I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)-I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\bm{\beta}},\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right).

(253)

Next, we focus on the two terms on the RHS of (
253
).
Following from similar ideas used in (
232
) and (
233
) with the difference that
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left(\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}}\right)\!\left(\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}}\right)^{H}\right]\!=\!\frac{p_{a}}{M}\mathbf{I}_{KML}
,
we have

I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)\leq L\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{p_{a}}{M}\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{KM}\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{KM}^{H}\right|\right].

(254)

Under the assumption that the entries of codebooks are i.i.d. with mean zero and variance
P
, the expectation on the RHS of (
254
) can be upper-bounded as

\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{p_{a}}{M}\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{KM}\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{KM}^{H}\right|\right]\leq\min\left\{n\log_{2}\left(1+p_{a}KP\right),KM\log_{2}\left(1+\frac{p_{a}}{M}nP\right)\right\}.

(255)

Moreover,
I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\bm{\beta}},\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)
can be computed as

\displaystyle I_{2}\left(\left.\bar{\mathbf{H}}\bar{\bm{\beta}};\bar{\mathbf{y}}\right|\bar{\bm{\beta}},\bar{\mathbf{X}}\right)

\displaystyle=L\;\mathbb{E}\left[\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}^{H}\right|\right]

(256)

\displaystyle=L\sum_{{\rm{K}}_{a}=0}^{K}\left(P_{K_{a}}({\rm{K}}_{a})\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\log_{2}\left|\mathbf{I}_{n}+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}^{H}\right|\right|K_{a}={\rm{K}}_{a}\right]\right),

(257)

where
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{K_{a}}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times K_{a}}
denotes a submatrix of
{\mathbf{X}}
formed by columns corresponding to the support of
\bar{\bm{\beta}}
;

P_{K_{a}}({\rm{K}}_{a})
denotes the probability of the event that there are exactly
{\rm K}_{a}
active users given in (
8
);
(
256
) follows from (
239
).
Combining (
253
), (
254
), and (
257
), the proof of the converse bound for the multiple-user case is completed.

I-B

Single-user random access converse bound

The converse bound for the scenario with knowledge of the activities of
K-1
potential users and the transmitted codewords and channel coefficients of active users among them, can be regarded as a converse bound for the massive random access problem.
In this case, it is equivalent to assume that there is a single user in the system with active probability
p_{a}
.
If this user is active, it equiprobably selects a message
W
from
\left\{1,2,\ldots,M\right\}
, and the corresponding codeword is denoted as
\mathbf{x}_{W}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
satisfying the maximum power constraint

\left\|\mathbf{x}_{W}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq nP.

(258)

Let
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
be a set of permissible channel inputs as specified by (
258
).
If this user is inactive, we assume
W=0
and
\mathbf{x}_{W}=\mathbf{0}
.
The received signal is given by

\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{x}_{W}\mathbf{h}^{T}+\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times L},

(259)

where the vector
\mathbf{h}\sim\mathcal{CN}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{L})
includes channel fading coefficients between the user and
L
antennas at the BS and the noise matrix
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times L}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,1)
entries.

Denote the decoded message as
\hat{W}\in\{0,1,\ldots,M\}
.
We define three types of error probabilities as follows:
the probability of the event that the receiver detects the presence of a message even though the user is inactive is given by

P_{e,1}=\mathbb{P}\left[\hat{W}\neq 0|W=0\right],

(260)

the probability of the event that the receiver does not decode correctly a transmitted message is given by

P_{e,2}=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m\in[M]}\mathbb{P}\left[\hat{W}\neq m|W=m\right],

(261)

and the probability of the event that the receiver erroneously decides that the user is inactive is given by

P_{e,3}=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m\in[M]}\mathbb{P}\left[\hat{W}=0|W=m\right],

(262)

where
P_{e,3}\leq P_{e,2}
.
Then, the error requirements in (
9
) and (
10
) can be rewritten as

P_{e,{\rm{MD}}}=p_{a}P_{e,2}\leq\epsilon_{\rm{MD}},

(263)

P_{e,{\rm{FA}}}=(1-p_{a})P_{e,1}\leq\epsilon_{\rm{FA}}.

(264)

An upper bound on the number of codewords that are compatible with the requirement that
P_{e,1}
,
P_{e,2}
, and
P_{e,3}
do not exceed
\epsilon_{1}
,
\epsilon_{2}
, and
\epsilon_{3}
, respectively, is provided in
[
18
, Theorem 2]
.
By changing the error requirement in
[
18
]
to (
263
) and (
264
) and by considering the multiple-receive-antenna setting, we obtain the following meta-converse result:

Proposition 19

Consider the single-user setup, where the user is active with probability
p_{a}
.
Let
Q_{Y^{n\times L}}
be an arbitrary distribution on
\mathcal{Y}^{n\times L}
.
When both the CSI and the user activity are unknown, every
(n,M,\epsilon_{\rm{MD}},\epsilon_{\rm{FA}},P)_{\text{no-CSI,no-}K_{a}}
code satisfies

M\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{P_{X^{n}}:\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{F}}\\
{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{3}\in[0,1]}\end{subarray}}\frac{1-\beta_{1-\epsilon_{1}}\left(P_{Y^{n\times L}|X^{n}=\mathbf{0}},Q_{Y^{n\times L}}\right)}{\beta_{1-\epsilon_{2}}\left(P_{X^{n}}P_{Y^{n\times L}|X^{n}},P_{X^{n}}Q_{Y^{n\times L}}\right)},

(265)

where

\beta_{1-\epsilon_{3}}\left(P_{Y^{n\times L}},Q_{Y^{n\times L}}\right)\leq 1-\beta_{1-\epsilon_{1}}\left(P_{Y^{n\times L}|X^{n}=\mathbf{0}},Q_{Y^{n\times L}}\right),

(266)

\epsilon_{3}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(267)

p_{a}\epsilon_{2}=\epsilon_{\rm{MD}},

(268)

(1-p_{a})\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{\rm{FA}}.

(269)

Proposition
19
presents a meta-converse bound for the single-user random access problem.
However, evaluating this bound is numerically intractable because it involves an optimization over all possible input distributions.
Next, we proceed to loosen Proposition
19
and obtain an easy-to-evaluate bound as provided in Theorem
10
.

Following from the inequality that
M_{m}(n,\epsilon,P)\leq M_{e}(n+1,\epsilon,P)

[
11
, Lemma 39]
, which relates the numbers of codewords under maximum power constraint and equal power constraint,
the condition in (
265
) can be loosened to

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{P_{X^{n+1}}:\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{F}^{n+1}}\\
{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{3}\in[0,1]}\end{subarray}}\frac{1-\beta_{1-\epsilon_{1}}\left(P_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}|X^{n+1}=\mathbf{0}},Q_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}\right)}{\beta_{1-\epsilon_{2}}\left(P_{X^{n+1}}P_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}|X^{n+1}},P_{X^{n+1}}Q_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}\right)}

(270)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{3}\in[0,1]}\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\beta_{1-\epsilon_{2}}\left(P_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}|X^{n+1}=\mathbf{x}_{1}},Q_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}\right)},

(271)

where
\mathcal{F}^{n+1}\!=\!\left\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{C}^{n+1}\!:\!\left\|\mathbf{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\!(n+1)P\right\}
,
the auxiliary distribution is chosen as
Q_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}=P_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}|X^{n+1}=\mathbf{0}}=\prod_{l=1}^{L}\mathcal{CN}(0,\mathbf{I}_{n+1})

[
42
]
,
and (
271
) follows from
[
11
, Lemma 29]
for any input
\mathbf{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{F}^{n+1}
.
Meanwhile, under
Q_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}=\prod_{l=1}^{L}\mathcal{CN}(0,\mathbf{I}_{n+1})
, the condition in (
266
) becomes

\beta_{1-\epsilon_{3}}\left(P_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}},Q_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}\right)\leq\epsilon_{1}.

(272)

Since
\alpha\mapsto\beta_{\alpha}\left(P_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}},Q_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}\right)
is monotonically nondecreasing, we can combine (
272
) and (
267
) as

\beta_{1-\epsilon_{2}}\left(P_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}},Q_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}\right)\leq\epsilon_{1}.

(273)

Following from
[
42
, Lemma 6]
, we can obtain that

\beta_{1-\epsilon_{2}}\left(P_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}},Q_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}\right)\leq M\beta_{1-\epsilon_{2}}\left(P_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}|X^{n+1}=\mathbf{x}_{1}},Q_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}\right).

(274)

Together with (
271
) and (
273
), we observe that the condition in (
273
) is satisfied once (
271
) is satisfied.

Next, we proceed to compute
\beta_{1-\epsilon_{2}}\left(P_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}|X^{n+1}=\mathbf{x}_{1}},Q_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}\right)
.
We have

\ln\frac{dP_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}|X^{n+1}=\mathbf{x}_{1}}}{dQ_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}}}=-L\ln(1+(n+1)P)+\sum_{l=1}^{L}\mathbf{y}_{l}^{H}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n+1}-(\mathbf{I}_{n+1}+\mathbf{x}_{1}\mathbf{x}_{1}^{H})^{-1}\right)\mathbf{y}_{l}.

(275)

Under
P_{Y^{(n+1)\times L}|X^{n+1}=\mathbf{x}_{1}}
, (
275
) is distributed the same as

H=-L\ln(1+(n+1)P)+(n+1)P\frac{\chi^{2}(2L)}{2}.

(276)

Under
